## Where the census round stands

Gate 6's readiness census counts, over the probes that drive the emulator through a pseudo-terminal, which spellings
of a prompt-readiness test they use and which probes have none; its pins are written only by
`tools/derive_hygiene_pins.sh` (owner rulings of 2026-09-23).  F2 -> F3 -> F4 -> **the F4 pass (panel
`panel_20260923_184642`, a dedicated codex job, the Opus measuring seat) -> THIS, F5.**  The gate's ROWS and the token
classifier's CODE are unchanged since F3; F4 and F5 changed only the deriver's input checks and comments.

## The F5 repair -- what changed (the full diff is inlined below)

1. **Names**: the allow-list is now COUNTED with `wc -c`.  F4 tested `$(...)` for emptiness, and command substitution
   strips trailing newlines, so a name holding a LINE FEED passed (measured: one argument grandfathered two names, one
   of them no file).
2. **`--ruling`**: an explicitly EMPTY ruling is refused; the date is parsed in UTC (`date -u -d`), because a local
   calendar can skip a midnight and GNU date then refuses a real day (measured under `TZ=America/Sao_Paulo`).
3. **The deriver runs the gate's census lines with the interpreter it found** (`python3` becomes a shell function
   calling it when `python3` itself does not run) and **proves the token classifier runs before trusting it**: a
   canary path that does not exist must come back listed.  Under git-bash `python3` is the Windows Store stub: the
   classifier printed nothing, all 43 grandfathered names read as GONE, and `--release` accepted a probe that is still
   promptless (the Opus seat, measured).
4. **The list writer anchors its search at line start** (`(?m)^GRANDFATHERED_PROMPTLESS="`, exactly one match), as the
   reader does: an unanchored search wrote into a COMMENT quoting the assignment and left a gate that no longer parses.
5. **`WHOLE_FULL_FILES`** -- the other double-quoted string the deriver writes FILE NAMES into -- gets the same byte
   allow-list (plus `:` and space for "name:count name:count"); it had none (grok).
6. **Comments**: the header's reason for refusing glob characters (they are inert inside double quotes; they expand in
   the gate's and the report's UNQUOTED printed lists); "a double quote", not "a quote"; the gate's helper sentence and
   its "cannot be evaded" / "closed-world" sentences (now: closed-world over the literal spellings on the PHYSICAL
   lines the comment filter keeps); the classifier docstring (a `match` class pattern fails OPEN; an alias call fails
   closed only if no other call is counted).

## §C — SETTLED PREMISES (recorded resolutions; say if the diff contradicts one)

1. The owner's rulings govern; the pins are written by the deriver, never by hand.
2. **The census is a SPELLING census**: it guarantees which spellings of a readiness test exist, not that a probe's
   readiness logic is right.
3. **The stopping rule**: in-round only a measured false pass or a wrong record in THIS round's material; pre-existing
   holes are FILED.  The deriver is new in this round, so all of it is this round's material.
4. The textual closed-world counts are LINE-based and drop every physical line beginning with `#`; the token
   classifier (`census_tokens.py`) is not a parser.

## §F2 — the F4 pass's findings, with MY disposition of each

*(Self-review disclosed: I wrote F2-F5 and these dispositions.  The F4 pass: grok, glm, deepseek, minimax and the kimi
substitute DO NOT SHIP; agy SHIP; the kimi CLI walled; **the codex panel seat is a SEAT FAILURE -- its answer ends in a
content-filter refusal ("flagged for possible cybersecurity risk") and the "verdict" the tally found is text it had
echoed from a file**; the dedicated codex job (plain wording) answered FAIL; the Opus seat -- claude-opus-5-5 throughout,
verified from its transcript -- DO NOT SHIP, by measurement.)*

**M1 -- a name holding a LINE FEED passed the allow-list** (codex-dedicated, grok, glm; the Opus seat measured it end to
end: `$'\nzz_bogus.py'` written, `$'zz-legit_probe.py\nextra_name.py'` wrote a name nobody checked).  **ACCEPTED -> wc -c.**
**M2 -- `--ruling ""` skipped validation** (codex-dedicated).  **ACCEPTED -> refused.**
**M3 -- a real date refused where the local calendar skips a midnight** (codex-dedicated read it; the Opus seat measured
it on WSL; git-bash's date ignores TZ).  **ACCEPTED -> UTC.**
**M4 -- under git-bash the gate's `python3` is the Store stub; the classifier printed nothing and `--release` accepted a
probe that is still promptless** (the Opus seat, overturning my K16, which was reasoned, not measured).  **ACCEPTED ->
the interpreter shim and the canary.**
**M5 -- the list writer's search was unanchored** (the Opus seat, latent: today the literal occurs once).  **ACCEPTED ->
anchored, exactly one match.**
**M6 -- `WHOLE_FULL_FILES` receives file names with no allow-list; a pty probe named with `$(...)` would become shell
source** (grok).  **ACCEPTED -> the same byte allow-list.**
**M7 -- the header's reason for refusing glob characters was false: they are inert in the double-quoted list** (grok,
deepseek, glm, the kimi substitute, the Opus seat).  **ACCEPTED -> the real reason**: the gate's and the report's
printed lists expand names UNQUOTED.  The refusal itself is kept (no probe name needs a glob character).
**M8 -- "a quote" over-covers the apostrophe, which is inert in both places** (the kimi substitute).  **ACCEPTED.**
**M9 -- the gate's "closed-world over the literal spellings" is false: the textual comment filter drops every physical
line beginning with `#`, so code on such a line (after a closing triple quote -- `hash_line`) and a file saved with
CR-only line endings (`cr_only`: grep sees one line, beginning `#!`) escape** (the Opus seat, measured).  **ACCEPTED as
records** (the sentence now says "on the physical lines the comment filter keeps"); **the holes are FILED** with `e10`,
widened from f-strings to the comment filter's whole class (pre-existing code).
**M10 -- K7's "counts as known" is imprecise: a bare `">"` literal is known and still fails the bare row** (the Opus
seat).  **ACCEPTED as records.**
**M11 -- K14 fails OPEN: a grandfathered probe gaining `case K.endswith("GXemul>"):` reads as no longer promptless, and
`--release` then takes it off the list with every row green** (the Opus seat, measured).  **DISCLOSED** in the docstring,
**FILED** (a token classifier is not a parser; an `ast`-based credit would close it -- a classifier change, its own round).
**M12 -- K15 is now a measured false pass** (the Opus seat: a probe moved to `newsub/readiness_predicate_test.py`
passes all 12 rows).  **FILED** (pre-existing), with the measurement.
**M13 -- K12's spaced-dot shape is e9's line-based class, not the whole-shape class; `n3g` (`buf .rstrip()`) is caught**
(grok).  **ACCEPTED** for the filing text.
**M14 -- `${RULING%%:*}` cuts at the LAST colon, so a reason holding a colon is refused** (minimax).  **REJECTED BY
MEASUREMENT** (above): `%%` cuts at the FIRST colon.

## §E -- QUESTIONS (answer each, numbered; from the packet alone, do Q1 and Q3 first)

**Q1 -- do the F5 checks refuse exactly what they say?**  (a) Every INPUT the deriver takes and writes into the gate:
is each one checked, and is each check byte-exact (no `$(...)` emptiness test, no line-based grep deciding a
multi-line value)?  Name any input I have not listed.  (b) The interpreter shim and the canary: can the classifier fail
in a way the canary does not see (it runs, prints the canary, and still misclassifies)?  (c) The anchored writer: can the
reader and the writer still disagree about which line is the list?
**Q2 -- the smallest input or tree state that gets past the F5 checks and does harm**: a ruling that lands anything but
comment lines, a pin moved without a warrant, a name or file name that changes meaning in the gate, or a census that
comes out empty or wrong while the deriver reports success.  If none, give your denominator.
**Q3 -- adjudicate M1-M16 and D3**: UPHOLD / OVERTURN / REFINE / CANNOT SETTLE, one line each with a reason.  Then:
**WHICH OF MY ACCEPTED DISPOSITIONS IS WRONG?**  In particular M9 and M11 (filed rather than fixed) and M14 (rejected).
**Q4 -- records**: is any sentence in the changed comments or the deriver's header false as of today?
**Q5 -- option-set completeness**: for M9 and M11, is there a disposition I did not name?

If, while verifying a claim, you found something ELSE -- say so.

End with a seat record: model, what you read, what you ran.


## OPERATIONAL RULE FOR EVERY SEAT (appended by panel.sh, not by the author)

**Do not run `run.sh`, any `gate_*.sh`, or the battery.** Every measurement you need is
inlined above. A gate takes minutes -- longer than your response budget -- and a second
concurrent invocation corrupts the operator run in progress, because the gates share one
log directory. A seat that runs a gate produces no review AND may invalidate someone
else s. Building and running a single detector or mutant in your own temp directory is
fine and encouraged; driving the harness is not.

If you want a measurement that is not inlined, NAME IT and say why. Naming it is worth
far more to this panel than a timeout.

   1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
   2: """census_tokens.py -- the TOKEN-level half of gate_hygiene.sh's readiness census (hygienecensus, 2026-09-23).
   3: 
   4: Why tokens.  The census's POSITIVE classifications -- "this probe has a prompt-readiness check", "this file holds N
   5: whole-buffer sites" -- were line-level greps, and text on a code line that is not code counted as code: a trailing
   6: comment, a string, a docstring.  Measured on the census round's F2 pass (the Opus seat): a probe whose only call of
   7: the ends-with method was a trailing comment or a docstring was counted as prompt-detecting, and two whole-buffer sites
   8: on ONE line counted as one.  Python's tokenizer separates COMMENT and STRING tokens from code, and every occurrence is
   9: a separate match, so neither can happen here.  (A probe in a subdirectory judged by a same-named top-level file was
  10: the third defect that pass measured; the GATE fixed that, by passing paths relative to regress/.)
  11: 
  12: Statement boundaries are KEPT.  The first version dropped NEWLINE tokens with the other layout tokens, so a method
  13: REFERENCE on one statement and a parenthesised string on the next -- `x = buf.rstrip().endswith` / `("GXemul>")` --
  14: read as one whole-buffer call, and a probe whose readiness test had been turned into that passed every row (measured by
  15: the census closing pass, controls_c2/t_nljoin).  NL, the newline INSIDE brackets, is still layout.  And a call is
  16: counted only where the name follows a "." -- a function DEFINITION under that name calls nothing, and the first
  17: version credited one (codex, the same pass).  Tokens are not a parse: a class PATTERN of the same shape in a `match`
  18: statement (`case K.<name>(...)`) is still counted, and a call through an alias with no "." before the name is not
  19: (the F3 pass, codex).  The second fails closed -- the probe is listed promptless, if no other call of it is counted.
  20: The first fails OPEN: a grandfathered probe that gains such a pattern reads as no longer promptless, and --release
  21: then takes it off the list with every row green (measured, the census F4 pass, the Opus seat; filed).
  22: 
  23: The CLOSED-WORLD counts (a bare prompt-suffix test, an unrecognised spelling of the ends-with call) are NOT done here
  24: and must not be: they count TEXT on code lines, in the gate itself, so a comment or a string that spells the method
  25: is counted -- the safe direction.  They see only the literal spelling with no space before the parenthesis (a space
  26: passes them: hygienecensusholes e9).  THIS FILE IS SCANNED BY THOSE COUNTS TOO (it is a top-level regress/*.py),
  27: which is why the method's name never appears here followed directly by an opening parenthesis -- not in code, not in
  28: this docstring.
  29: 
  30: Usage (paths are read one per line from stdin, relative to the directory given):
  31:     census_tokens.py promptless DIR   print each path with no method call of the ends-with method: the OP token ".",
  32:                                       a NAME token spelled ENDS_WITH, the OP token "(", in one statement.  A file that
  33:                                       cannot be read or tokenized is PRINTED (listed promptless: it cannot be read, so it
  34:                                       is not trusted).  Tokens are not a parse: a file that tokenizes but would not
  35:                                       compile is graded like any other.
  36:     census_tokens.py whole DIR        print "path:N" for each path with N > 0 code-level whole-buffer sites: a NAME
  37:                                       whose last three letters are "buf", then ".", "rstrip", "(", ")", ".", ENDS_WITH,
  38:                                       "(", a STRING whose value is exactly "GXemul>", ")", in one statement.  Counted
  39:                                       per OCCURRENCE, either quote, any spacing between the tokens.  An unreadable file
  40:                                       prints NOTHING here (it has no count); the promptless mode is what names it.
  41: Exit status 2 on a usage error; otherwise 0 -- an unreadable path is an output (promptless) or a missing count (whole),
  42: never an exception.  On Python 3.12 and later an f-string's replacement fields are tokenized as code, so a call inside
  43: one counts; on earlier versions it is one STRING token and does not (no probe relies on either).
  44: """
  45: import ast, io, os, sys, tokenize
  46: 
  47: ENDS_WITH = "ends" + "with"          # spelled in two halves: see the docstring's CLOSED-WORLD paragraph
  48: SKIP = (tokenize.NL, tokenize.INDENT, tokenize.DEDENT, tokenize.COMMENT, tokenize.ENCODING)   # NEWLINE is KEPT
  49: 
  50: 
  51: def code_tokens(path):
  52:     src = io.open(path, encoding="utf-8", errors="strict").read()
  53:     return [t for t in tokenize.generate_tokens(io.StringIO(src).readline) if t.type not in SKIP]
  54: 
  55: 
  56: def has_ends_with_call(toks):
  57:     return any(d.type == tokenize.OP and d.string == "." and a.type == tokenize.NAME and a.string == ENDS_WITH
  58:                and b.type == tokenize.OP and b.string == "("
  59:                for d, a, b in zip(toks, toks[1:], toks[2:]))
  60: 
  61: 
  62: def is_prompt_string(t):
  63:     if t.type != tokenize.STRING:
  64:         return False
  65:     try:
  66:         return ast.literal_eval(t.string) == "GXemul>"
  67:     except (ValueError, SyntaxError):
  68:         return False
  69: 
  70: 
  71: SHAPE = [(tokenize.OP, "."), (tokenize.NAME, "rstrip"), (tokenize.OP, "("), (tokenize.OP, ")"), (tokenize.OP, "."),
  72:          (tokenize.NAME, ENDS_WITH), (tokenize.OP, "("), (tokenize.STRING, None), (tokenize.OP, ")")]
  73: 
  74: 
  75: def whole_sites(toks):
  76:     n = 0
  77:     for i in range(len(toks) - len(SHAPE)):
  78:         head = toks[i]
  79:         if head.type != tokenize.NAME or head.string[-3:] != "buf":
  80:             continue
  81:         rest = toks[i + 1:i + 1 + len(SHAPE)]
  82:         if all(t.type == kind and (text is None or t.string == text) for (kind, text), t in zip(SHAPE, rest)) \
  83:                 and is_prompt_string(rest[7]):
  84:             n += 1
  85:     return n
  86: 
  87: 
  88: def main(argv):
  89:     if len(argv) != 3 or argv[1] not in ("promptless", "whole"):
  90:         sys.stderr.write(__doc__)
  91:         return 2
  92:     mode, base = argv[1], argv[2]
  93:     for line in sys.stdin.read().split("\n"):
  94:         rel = line.strip("\r")
  95:         if not rel:
  96:             continue
  97:         try:
  98:             toks = code_tokens(os.path.join(base, rel))
  99:         except (OSError, ValueError, UnicodeDecodeError, tokenize.TokenError, SyntaxError, IndentationError):
 100:             if mode == "promptless":
 101:                 print(rel)
 102:             continue
 103:         if mode == "promptless":
 104:             if not has_ends_with_call(toks):
 105:                 print(rel)
 106:         else:
 107:             n = whole_sites(toks)
 108:             if n:
 109:                 print("%s:%d" % (rel, n))
 110:     return 0

 114:     sys.exit(main(sys.argv))

  75:     --release) MODE=release; shift; GF=("$@") ;;

  78: esac
  79: if [ -n "$RULING" ]; then
  80:     #  PRINTABLE ASCII ONLY, byte by byte: `grep` tests LINES, so a newline let a valid first line carry live shell
  81:     #  into the gate (the census closing pass, measured), and a [[:cntrl:]] test then let through what the locale does
  82:     #  not call a control -- a non-breaking space, U+0085, U+2028 (the F3 pass; each stayed inside the comment line).
  83:     [ $(( $(printf '%s' "$RULING" | LC_ALL=C tr -d ' -~' | wc -c) )) -eq 0 ] \
  84:         || { echo "REFUSED: the ruling text must be printable ASCII, on one line"; exit 2; }
  85:     printf '%s' "$RULING" | grep -qE '^[0-9]{4}-[0-9]{2}-[0-9]{2}: .{16,}$' \
  86:         || { echo "REFUSED: a ruling reads \"YYYY-MM-DD: <who ruled and why>\" (16+ characters of reason) -- got [$RULING]"; exit 2; }
  87:     _day=${RULING%%:*}
  88:     #  in UTC: a local calendar can skip a midnight (a DST change), and GNU date then refuses a real day (the F4 pass)
  89:     [ "$(date -u -d "$_day" +%F 2>/dev/null)" = "$_day" ] || { echo "REFUSED: [$_day] is not a calendar date"; exit 2; }
  90:     case "$RULING" in *'"'*|*'\'*|*'$'*|*'`'*) echo "REFUSED: the ruling text may not contain a double quote, a backslash, \$ or a backtick"; exit 2 ;; esac
  91:     _why=$(printf '%s' "${RULING#*: }" | LC_ALL=C tr -cd 'A-Za-z0-9')
  92:     [ ${#_why} -ge 16 ] || { echo "REFUSED: a ruling needs at least 16 letters or digits of reason -- got [$RULING]"; exit 2; }
  93: fi

  95: fn() {  # extract a shell function definition by name: from `name() {` through the first line ending in `}`
  96:     awk -v n="$1" 'index($0, n "() {")==1 {p=1} p {print} p && /}[[:space:]]*$/ {exit}' "$G"
  97: }
  98: asg() { # extract an assignment by variable name: continue while the line ends in a backslash OR a single quote is
  99:         # still open (the gate writes `tr ' ' '<newline>'` with a LITERAL newline inside the quotes)
 100:     awk -v n="$1" 'index($0, n "=")==1 {p=1}
 101:         p {print; q += gsub(/\047/, "\047"); if ($0 !~ /\\$/ && q % 2 == 0) exit}' "$G"
 102: }

 106:     CODE="$CODE$d"$'\n'

 126: spin() { sed -n "s/^$1=\"\(.*\)\"$/\1/p" "$G" | head -1; }

 131: NEWP=$(LC_ALL=C comm -13 <(printf '%s\n' "$GFS") <(printf '%s\n' "$_promptless" | grep .))

 156:     #  a name given twice was appended twice (45 entries, 44 names) and --release then could not remove it -- the

 159:     [ -z "$dup" ] || { echo "REFUSED: a name given more than once: $dup"; exit 4; }

 163:         #  name matched the blank line an empty NEWP prints (the F3 pass).  So an allow-list, COUNTED byte by byte with
 164:         #  wc -c: the F4 version tested $(...) for emptiness, and command substitution strips trailing newlines, so a
 165:         #  name holding a LINE FEED passed and grandfathered two names at once, one of them no file (the F4 pass).

 171:         else

 173:         fi
 174:     done

 176: import io, re, sys
 177: g, mode, names = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2], sys.argv[3:]
 178: s = io.open(g, encoding="utf-8", newline="").read()
 179: #  anchored at line start, as the reader above is: an unanchored search would write into the first COMMENT that
 180: #  quotes the assignment and leave a gate that no longer parses (the census F4 pass, the Opus seat)
 181: ms = list(re.finditer(r'(?m)^GRANDFATHERED_PROMPTLESS="', s))
 182: assert len(ms) == 1, "GRANDFATHERED_PROMPTLESS assignments at line start: %d" % len(ms)
 183: i = ms[0].end(); j = s.index('"', i)
 184: body = s[i:j]
 185: if mode == "grandfather":
 186:     body = body + "\n" + " ".join(names)
 187: else:
 188:     #  EXACT names, per line: a boundary regex matched `foo.py` inside `pref-foo.py` (codex, the closing pass)
 189:     lines = [l.split() for l in body.split("\n")]
 190:     for n in names:
 191:         k = sum(l.count(n) for l in lines)
 192:         assert k == 1, (n, k)
 193:         lines = [[w for w in l if w != n] for l in lines]
 194:     body = "\n".join(" ".join(l) for l in lines if l)
 195: io.open(g, "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="").write(s[:i] + body + s[j:])
 196: print("%s: %s" % ("GRANDFATHERED" if mode == "grandfather" else "RELEASED", " ".join(names)))
 197: PY
 198:     echo "DERIVE_DONE"
 199:     exit 0
 200: fi
 201: if [ "$MODE" = write ]; then
 202:     #  WHOLE_FULL_FILES is the other place this tool writes FILE NAMES into a double-quoted string in the gate, and it
 203:     #  had no allow-list: a pty file named with `$(...)` would have become shell source there on the next read (grok,
 204:     #  the F4 pass).  The same bytes as a grandfathered name, plus the ':' and the space of "name:count name:count":
 205:     [ $(( $(printf '%s' "$whole_files" | LC_ALL=C tr -d 'A-Za-z0-9._/: -' | wc -c) )) -eq 0 ] \
 206:         || { echo "REFUSED: a whole-buffer file name holds a byte outside A-Za-z0-9._/- -- rename the probe: [$whole_files]"; exit 5; }
 207:     [ "$conv_anchor" = "$conv_echo" ] && [ "$conv_echo" = "$conv_mark" ] || {
 208:         echo "REFUSED: the three #392 counts disagree ($conv_anchor/$conv_echo/$conv_mark) -- a guard present but uncounted; fix the probe, not the pin"; exit 5; }
 209:     [ -z "$NEWP" ] || { echo "REFUSED: new promptless probes must be grandfathered by NAME (--grandfather) or given prompt detection first"; exit 5; }
 210:     [ -z "$GONE" ] || { echo "REFUSED: grandfathered names are no longer promptless -- --release them first"; exit 5; }
 211:     [ "$bare" -le "$(pin EXPECT_BARE)" ] || { echo "REFUSED: bare endswith(\">\") grew -- a defect, never re-pinned"; exit 5; }
 212:     [ "$unknown" -le "$(pin EXPECT_UNKNOWN)" ] || { echo "REFUSED: an unrecognised endswith( spelling appeared -- a defect, never re-pinned"; exit 5; }
 213:     NEED=""
 214:     [ "$conv_anchor" -ge "$(pin EXPECT_CONVERTED)" ] || NEED="$NEED EXPECT_CONVERTED($(pin EXPECT_CONVERTED)->$conv_anchor)"
 215:     [ "$whole_full" = "$(pin EXPECT_WHOLE_FULL)" ] || NEED="$NEED EXPECT_WHOLE_FULL($(pin EXPECT_WHOLE_FULL)->$whole_full)"
 216:     [ "$whole_files" = "$WFP" ] || NEED="$NEED WHOLE_FULL_FILES([$WFP]->[$whole_files])"
 217:     if [ -n "$NEED" ] && [ -z "$RULING" ]; then
 218:         echo "REFUSED: these pins change only by a named decision --$NEED -- a #392 guard removed, or a whole-buffer site added, moved, respelled or converted; fix the probe, or pass --ruling \"YYYY-MM-DD: <who ruled and why>\""
 219:         exit 5
 220:     fi
 221:     pywrite "$G" "$bare" "$unknown" "$_ptyn" "$conv_anchor" "$whole_full" "$whole_files" "$RULING" "$NEED" <<'PY'
 222: import io, re, sys
 223: g, bare, unk, pty, conv, whole, files, ruling, need = sys.argv[1:]
 224: s = io.open(g, encoding="utf-8", newline="").read()
 225: note = ("#  RULING %s -- %s (tools/derive_hygiene_pins.sh --write)\n" % (ruling, need.strip())) if (ruling and need.strip()) else ""
 226: for k, v in (("EXPECT_BARE", bare), ("EXPECT_UNKNOWN", unk), ("EXPECT_PTY_PROBES", pty), ("EXPECT_CONVERTED", conv),
 227:              ("EXPECT_WHOLE_FULL", whole)):
 228:     pre = note if (note and k + "(" in need) else ""
 229:     s, n = re.subn(r"(?m)^%s=\d+" % k, lambda m, k=k, v=v, pre=pre: pre + "%s=%s" % (k, v), s, count=1)
 230:     assert n == 1, k
 231: pre = note if (note and "WHOLE_FULL_FILES(" in need) else ""
 232: s, n = re.subn(r'(?m)^WHOLE_FULL_FILES="[^"\n]*"', lambda m: pre + 'WHOLE_FULL_FILES="%s"' % files, s, count=1)
 233: assert n == 1, "WHOLE_FULL_FILES"
 234: io.open(g, "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="").write(s)
 235: print("WRITTEN: EXPECT_BARE=%s EXPECT_UNKNOWN=%s EXPECT_PTY_PROBES=%s EXPECT_CONVERTED=%s EXPECT_WHOLE_FULL=%s "
 236:       "WHOLE_FULL_FILES=\"%s\"%s" % (bare, unk, pty, conv, whole, files, ("  RULING: " + ruling) if note else ""))
 237: PY
 238: fi

   1: #!/bin/bash
   2: # GATE 6 -- no distress markers in the boot logs.
   3: #
   4: # Cheap, and it has caught real regressions: round 40 found that #265's own diagnostic
   5: # flooded the log on a healthy boot, and that #261 was listing a subsystem it had not
   6: # actually armed. Neither changed a pass/fail count -- both showed up only as marker counts.
   7: #
   8: # READS THE RAW PTY LOGS ON PURPOSE. fatal() and debugmsg() output is not guest console
   9: # output; it goes straight to stdout and interleaves into the pty stream, and the arc
  10: # screen reconstruction that gate 4 needs for GUEST tokens actively garbles it. Measured:
  11: # '{ asc: data in' reads 0 from the arc screen rebuild and 5 from the raw log.
  12: #
  13: # TWO WAYS THIS GATE USED TO PASS WITHOUT PROVING ANYTHING, both now closed:
  14: #
  15: #  * EMPTY LOGS. It checked only that the files existed. `: > /tmp/pD.log` made 20 checks
  16: #    go green -- two empty files certifying a clean boot. There is now a POSITIVE CONTROL:
  17: #    each log must be substantial AND contain the guest's own uid=0(root), so absence of
  18: #    distress is only counted when presence of a real boot is proved.
  19: #  * STALE LOGS. Everything lives in /tmp and survives until reboot, so running this gate
  20: #    alone -- or after gate 4 skipped -- graded yesterday's logs, produced by a binary that
  21: #    no longer exists. Gate 4 now deletes them first and copies them into $LOGDIR when it
  22: #    finishes; this gate reads THOSE copies, which only exist if gate 4 actually ran.
  23: set -u
  24: HERE=$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)
  25: . "$HERE/lib.sh"
  26: 
  27: gate_begin "log-hygiene"
  28: 
  29: # ---- static wiring: the readiness-predicate ratchet ---------------------
  30: #
  31: #  DELIBERATELY BEFORE the log check below, so a missing log can never SKIP
  32: #  it.  This gate had no source assertions at all until now -- it graded logs
  33: #  and nothing else -- which is why the defect it guards could spread unseen.
  34: #
  35: #  THE DEFECT IT FREEZES: a readiness predicate matching a BARE ">" is
  36: #  satisfied by the guest's own register dump, whose FIRST line is the
  37: #  symbol line "  <...>" -- and that line ends in ">".  Line-buffered on a
  38: #  pty, each line is its own flush, so a reader waking between line 1 and
  39: #  line 2 returns SATISFIED WITH THE REGISTERS UNREAD.  Healthy host,
  40: #  microseconds wide, and armed on every CPU family whose dump starts that
  41: #  way (MIPS, PPC, SH, m88k all do).
  42: #
  43: #  THE CONVERSION HAS LANDED (#392), so EXPECT_BARE is now 0 and this is a
  44: #  RATCHET AGAINST REGRESSION rather than against spread.  All 15 sites now take
  45: #  a fresh mark before the write, require the FULL prompt in that slice, and
  46: #  require the command's own echo first.
  47: #
  48: #  The converted predicate deliberately keeps the LITERAL endswith("GXemul>")
  49: #  spelling, which is the idiom the ARM probes already use (arm_idle_probe.py:194).
  50: #  A reviewer proposed a shared helper holding the prompt in a constant or a
  51: #  regex; either would have broken this gate, though -- as a pass-2 seat pointed
  52: #  out -- NOT by the same route, and the first draft of this comment conflated
  53: #  them:
  54: #      endswith(PROMPT)  -> counted, unrecognised -> fails the `unknown` check;
  55: #      re.search(...)    -> not an endswith at all, so `unknown` never moves --
  56: #                           it fails the anchored-site COUNT below instead.
  57: #  Matching the house idiom kept the ratchet working with no redesign, which is
  58: #  worth remembering the next time a "cleaner" abstraction is proposed for
  59: #  something a static check is watching.
  60: #
  61: #  Exact equality, not a ceiling, and on purpose: a "<=" check would let the
  62: #  number fall silently, and a silent fall is how you lose track of whether
  63: #  the conversion actually happened.  Any movement in either direction must
  64: #  be a deliberate edit here.
  65: #  COUNTING IT PROPERLY, after a review pass named three ways to walk around a
  66: #  naive grep: the single-quoted spelling, a variable or helper holding the
  67: #  prompt, and probes living in a subdirectory the flat glob never visits.
  68: #  A ratchet you can step around is worse than no ratchet, because it reads as
  69: #  protection.  So:
  70: #    - find RECURSIVELY, not "$HERE"/*.py;
  71: #    - accept either quote style;
  72: #    - and make the check CLOSED-WORLD: every .endswith() in the tree must use
  73: #      one of the two KNOWN literals.  A third spelling -- endswith(PROMPT),
  74: #      say -- is unknown, and unknown fails.  A helper module under regress/
  75: #      is scanned like a probe: an endswith( line there with a known literal
  76: #      is not "unknown" (the bare one still fails the bare row), and one with
  77: #      an unrecognised argument fails "unknown".  What no count here
  78: #      sees is WHICH probe a helper's test serves: a probe whose ONLY test
  79: #      moved into a helper is listed promptless and the names row reds
  80: #      (fail-closed), while a probe that keeps one call of its own can move
  81: #      the rest into a helper with every row green (hygienecensusholes n3k,
  82: #      n3k2; this said a helper's line was unknown until the census F3 pass,
  83: #      codex and grok).  That converts "I grepped for the bad thing" into "I
  84: #      enumerated everything and recognised all of it" -- for the spellings
  85: #      these counts can SEE.
  86: #      (*** 2026-09-23: this said it was "the only form that cannot be evaded
  87: #      by inventing a new way to write it".  FALSE: every count here is LINE-
  88: #      based and keys on the literal `endswith(`, so a space before the paren
  89: #      (`endswith (">")`) or a backslash continuation between the name and
  90: #      the paren removes a call from all of them (both measured on copies,
  91: #      controls_f2 e_e9 and controls_c5 t_bsl).  Filed as hygienecensusholes
  92: #      e9, ranked first; the census F3 pass widened it from one spelling to
  93: #      this LINE-based class (glm, agy, the kimi substitute).  And the
  94: #      comment filter drops every PHYSICAL line that begins with `#`: code on
  95: #      such a line (after a closing triple quote, or in an f-string field) and
  96: #      a whole file saved with CR-only line endings (grep then sees one line,
  97: #      beginning `#!`) escape too -- measured, the census F4 pass (the Opus
  98: #      seat: hash_line, cr_only), filed with e10.  The census is closed-world
  99: #      over the literal spellings on the physical lines it keeps, not over
 100: #      the method. ***)
 101: EXPECT_BARE=0
 102: EXPECT_UNKNOWN=0
 103: #  CODE ONLY.  The first version of this check counted a line of PROSE -- a
 104: #  comment in arm_flags_probe.py that discusses endswith() -- and reported an
 105: #  unrecognised spelling that does not exist.  A check that fires on its own
 106: #  documentation trains the reader to ignore it, so full-line comments are
 107: #  stripped before anything is counted.  (Known limit, stated rather than
 108: #  hidden: an endswith() sitting after code on the same line as a trailing
 109: #  comment is still counted as code, which is the safe direction.)
 110: #  readiness_predicate_test.py is EXCLUDED here and pinned separately below.
 111: #  It is the offline truth table for #392, so it necessarily CONTAINS the two
 112: #  broken spellings -- demonstrating them is its whole job. Counting them with
 113: #  the live sites would make the ratchet permanently red.
 114: #
 115: #  This is a FAIL-CLOSED exception, not a hole: the file is named exactly, and
 116: #  its own contents are asserted below, so it cannot quietly stop testing what it
 117: #  claims to test. A loose `grep -v test` would have been the wrong shape -- it
 118: #  would silently exempt any future file with "test" in its name.
 119: #  *** SCOPE, MEASURED 2026-08-29: `regress/artifacts/` IS PRESERVED EVIDENCE, NOT LIVE
 120: #  HARNESS CODE, and leaving it in scope was a defect in BOTH directions. ***

 122: #  *** THE NUMBERS THAT WERE HERE WENT STALE INSIDE THIS VERY DIFF, so they are

 125: #  in a comment ABOUT counting, wrong before the commit that wrote it had landed.
 126: #  Caught by the codex seat. ***
 127: #
 128: #      find . -name "*.py" | wc -l                             the OLD recursive scope
 129: #      find . -name "*.py" -not -path "*/artifacts/*" | wc -l  the NEW one
 130: #      ls *.py | wc -l                                         the flat glob
 131: #      grep -l "pty.fork" artifacts/*/*.py artifacts/*/*/*.py  the invisible copies
 132: #
 133: #  THE LOAD-BEARING FACT IS THE EQUALITY, NOT THE VALUES: recursive-minus-artifacts
 134: #  equals the flat glob, which is what makes this the right exclusion -- it ALIGNS the
 135: #  censuses instead of moving any of them.  Re-derive rather than quote; if the two
 136: #  ever differ, that difference is the finding.
 137: #
 138: #  Two censuses recursed while a third used a flat glob -- which is evasion route #3
 139: #  that this file condemns ninety lines above.  TEN pty-forking promptless probes sat
 140: #  under artifacts/, invisible to the denominator, to the promptless pin and to the
 141: #  comm -13 offender-naming: invisible to the entire mechanism built to find them.
 142: #
 143: #  AND THE HAZARD WAS SCHEDULED IN THE OTHER DIRECTION.  CLAUDE.md MANDATES frozen
 144: #  probe copies for a comparison run; copying one converted probe into artifacts/
 145: #  takes conv_anchor 23 -> 24 and reds this gate -- so the next round to freeze a
 146: #  probe would have gone red FOR OBEYING THE CARRIER.
 147: #
 148: #  RECURSION IS KEPT so a genuinely new subdirectory is still scanned; only the
 149: #  evidence archive is excluded.  Measured a NO-OP on every pinned count today.
 150: py_code() { find "$HERE" -name '*.py' -type f ! -path "$HERE/artifacts/*" \
 151:                  ! -name 'readiness_predicate_test.py' -print0 2>/dev/null | \
 152:             xargs -0 grep -h "endswith(" 2>/dev/null | grep -v '^[[:space:]]*#'; }
 153: #  -o, so these count OCCURRENCES and not LINES.  With -c a single line holding
 154: #  two different spellings counts once in each of the three totals and drives
 155: #  `unknown` NEGATIVE -- a subtraction is only meaningful if all three terms
 156: #  count the same kind of thing.
 157: bare=$(py_code | grep -o "endswith([\"']>[\"'])" | wc -l)
 158: #  Every endswith( in code, minus the two we recognise: the bare prompt above
 159: #  and the correct full prompt.  Anything left is a spelling nobody reviewed --
 160: #  including endswith(PROMPT), wherever it sits under regress/ (a helper module included).
 161: allends=$(py_code | grep -o "endswith(" | wc -l)
 162: full=$(py_code | grep -o "endswith([\"']GXemul>[\"'])" | wc -l)
 163: unknown=$((allends - bare - full))
 164: check "readiness: bare-prompt sites frozen (#37)" "$bare" "$EXPECT_BARE"
 165: check "readiness: no unrecognised endswith spelling" "$unknown" "$EXPECT_UNKNOWN"
 166: 
 167: #  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 168: #  *** THE THREE COUNTS ABOVE ARE CLOSED-WORLD AND STRUCTURALLY CANNOT SEE A
 169: #  PROBE WITH NO PROMPT DETECTION AT ALL. ***  py_code() concatenates every
 170: #  `endswith(` line across the tree, so a file containing ZERO of them
 171: #  contributes to no counter and passes invisibly.  A ratchet built to catch
 172: #  probes with BAD prompt detection is blind to probes with NONE -- which is
 173: #  the larger set: SEVEN of the 33 pty-forking probes have no prompt
 174: #  detection whatever.
 175: #
 176: #  The fix is the one this project applies to every phantom-zero: ENUMERATE THE
 177: #  DENOMINATOR FIRST.  Count the pty-forking probes, then the promptless subset,
 178: #  and compare that subset against an explicit list.
 179: #
 180: #  *** IT IS A RATCHET, NOT A REJECT, AND THAT IS NOT A COMPROMISE. ***  EVERY
 181: #  current probe is promptless, so "reject any file lacking a predicate" would
 182: #  redden the whole battery PERMANENTLY -- a red no edit can clear, which this
 183: #  project has already paid for once and which destroys the credibility of every
 184: #  other row in the run.  The grandfathered set is named exactly, so it cannot
 185: #  quietly grow; a NEW promptless probe fails, and converting one is a
 186: #  ratchet-down that must edit this list.
 187: #
 188: #  Provenance: a codex seat named the enumerate-first shape on the
 189: #  probetimebased panel; the ratchet-not-reject refinement is the adjudication's,
 190: #  after it checked what a reject-all would do to the current set.
 191: #  *** BOTH PINS BELOW WERE RED AT HEAD UNTIL 2026-08-27, FOR THE SAME CAUSE, AND NOBODY
 192: #  HAD SEEN IT. ***  Derived with this gate's own commands, not estimated:
 193: #
 194: #      EXPECT_PTY_PROBES   pinned 33   actual 35
 195: #      grandfathered set   pinned  7   actual  9
 196: #
 197: #  The two extra files are `asic_badaddr_probe.py` (#463) and `wdc_flood_probe.py` (#464).
 198: #  Both were added AFTER the pins were last set and neither pin was bumped.  The weekly
 199: #  battery runs Sundays, so the red had not surfaced -- and when it did it would have been
 200: #  mis-attributed to whatever change triggered that run.  That is the same class #486 closed
 201: #  for `overrun x2` on the same day: a scored row red for an unrelated, already-diagnosed
 202: #  reason.  Found by a panel seat re-deriving the pin rather than reading it.
 203: #
 204: #  *** ADDING THEM TO THE GRANDFATHERED SET IS NOT A BYPASS, AND THE DISTINCTION IS THE
 205: #  WHOLE POINT OF THE ROW. ***  This list is not `probes we forgive`; it is `promptless
 206: #  probes we KNOW about`, and the check's job is to NAME any that appear unnoticed (the
 207: #  `comm -13` below).  Both new entries are promptless for exactly the reason the five
 208: #  already listed are -- they free-run to a breakpoint, and only a breakpoint hit returns a
 209: #  prompt, as `sh_intprio_probe.py` records in its own comment.  So they belong in the set,
 210: #  with the reason written down rather than the count quietly raised.
 211: #
 212: #  *** THAT STATED REASON WAS WRONG ABOUT ITS OWN SET, AND IT SENT A READER TO THE WRONG
 213: #  ONE OF THE TWO DISPOSITIONS THIS ROW OFFERS.  Censused member by member 2026-08-29,
 214: #  independently by three seats (codex, opus5, and the main loop), all agreeing: ***
 215: #
 216: #  *** THESE CATEGORIES OVERLAP.  THEY ARE NOT A PARTITION, AND AN EARLIER DRAFT OF
 217: #  THIS BLOCK PRINTED THEM AS ONE -- 3 + 8 + 1 = 12 over a NINE-member set.  Caught on
 218: #  pass 2 by the glm and agy seats, on the arithmetic. ***
 219: #

 221: #        of which ALSO free-run to a breakpoint  3       sh_intprio, sh_iprbcd, wdc_flood
 222: #      NEITHER ............................... 0
 223: #

 225: #  SHAPE.  Recorded because the third one is the instructive one. ***

 227: #        breakpoint") that was true of 3 members.
 228: #    (2) MY FIRST CORRECTION printed 3 + 8 + 1 = 12 against a NINE-member set --
 229: #        it read as a PARTITION when the categories overlap.  Caught on the
 230: #        arithmetic by the glm and agy seats.
 231: #    (3) MY SECOND CORRECTION said drive_guest.py is NEITHER.  It is not: it
 232: #        budget-drains at `expect()` and its sibling, spelled
 233: #        `while time.time() - t < timeout:`.  I had grepped for the literal
 234: #        `time.time() < end`, which is ONE SPELLING of the mechanism, and
 235: #        measured the spelling instead of the thing.  Caught by opus5.
 236: #
 237: #  *** THAT IS THIS ROUND'S OWN DEFECT, COMMITTED IN THE COMMENT DESCRIBING IT:
 238: #  counting a TEXTUAL FORM where a PROPERTY was meant. ***  It is why the row-5
 239: #  replacement counts set/read BALANCE rather than occurrences of a spelling.
 240: #
 241: #  drive_guest.py IS in the promptless list, and for a reason that is about the

 243: #  `re.search`, which `grep -L "endswith("` cannot see.  That is why it is
 244: #  LISTED -- not how it waits.  The two axes were conflated by (3).
 245: #

 247: #  budget-drain, so the set is covered by budget-drain alone and "neither" is empty.
 248: #
 249: #  So the mechanism ALL ELEVEN share is BUDGET-DRAIN --
 250: #  readiness by elapsed time rather than by prompt -- and the two probes added below share

 255: #  classified promptless only because `grep -L "endswith("` cannot see REGEX readiness:

 257: #  anticipates that -- "re.search(...) -> not an endswith at all, so `unknown` never

 259: #  universal claim for another.  A per-member reason, not a dominant one.
 260: #
 261: #  The grandfather-with-a-reason discipline is UNCHANGED; only the reason is repaired.

 410: GRANDFATHERED_PROMPTLESS="pcc2_width_witness.py pcc2_width_probe.py sgi_de_primop_probe.py drive_guest.py gdrom_data_probe.py gdrom_default_probe.py

 419: #  hygienecensus: tools/derive_hygiene_pins.sh --grandfather appended THREE blocks to the list above; they are
 420: #  named here by their FIRST NAME, never by position, because the next --grandfather appends another.
 421: #  The block beginning `alpha_harness_probe.py` (derived 2026-09-19): 20 PROMPTLESS pty probes committed

 432: #  classifier reading full-line comments (it reads tokens since the F2 repair).  All three are UNTRACKED and lying
 433: #  in the tree, named so the census passes on
