 435: #  `--grandfather NAME...` / `--release NAME...`, then `--write`.
 436: 
 437: #  *** THIS WAS THE THIRD POPULATION AND THE F2 EDIT MISSED IT.  Found by the agy seat
 438: #  on pass 2. ***  py_code and probe_code were converted to recursive-minus-artifacts
 439: #  while this line kept a FLAT GLOB -- so two censuses were aligned and a third was not,
 440: #  which is the exact defect F2 exists to end.
 441: #
 442: #  It AGREES today (recursive-minus-artifacts == flat == 59) and that agreement is a
 443: #  COINCIDENCE, not a property: a .py added in any NEW non-artifacts subdirectory would
 444: #  be scanned by two censuses and missed by this one.  Equal counts are not an aligned
 445: #  population, and reading the equality as safety is how the flat glob survived the
 446: #  first repair.
 447: #  hygienecensus F2: paths RELATIVE TO regress/, not basenames -- a probe in a subdirectory was classified by a
 448: #  same-named top-level file (measured: a promptless copy at newsub/ read as prompt-detecting), and a bare xargs
 449: #  split a filename holding a space.  NUL-safe as far as grep: `grep -l` prints one name per LINE, so a file name
 450: #  holding a newline is not supported (and a name holding a space cannot be grandfathered -- the list is
 451: #  space-separated; the deriver refuses it by name).  For every file in regress/ itself the relative path IS the basename, so the
 452: #  grandfathered names are unchanged.
 453: _ptylist=$(find "$HERE" -name "*.py" -type f ! -path "$HERE/artifacts/*" -print0 2>/dev/null \
 454:            | xargs -0 grep -l "pty.fork" 2>/dev/null | sed "s|^$HERE/||" | LC_ALL=C sort)
 455: _ptyn=$(printf '%s' "$_ptylist" | grep -c . )
 456: #  hygienecensus (2026-09-23): THE CLASSIFIER READS PYTHON TOKENS -- regress/census_tokens.py, where a COMMENT, a
 457: #  STRING or a DOCSTRING is not code.  It was a raw `grep -L "endswith("`, so text naming the method hid a
 458: #  promptless probe: crime_width_probe.py's only such text was a comment this very round added (pass 2).  The
 459: #  first repair stripped only FULL-LINE comments and said it "reads code" -- the F2 pass measured seven edits, on
 460: #  five probe files, where a TRAILING comment, a string or a docstring changed the answer (the Opus seat's n1a-n1d,
 461: #  n2a, n2b, n9).  A comment, a string or a docstring is never a "." NAME "(" sequence of tokens, so none of
 462: #  those can hide a probe now; and the classifier keeps STATEMENT boundaries, so a method reference on one line
 463: #  and a parenthesised string on the next is not a call (the closing pass, codex -- controls_c2/t_nljoin).  A file that cannot be read or tokenized is listed promptless (it cannot
 464: #  be read, so it is not trusted).  The spelling counts above deliberately stay textual, so a comment or a string
 465: #  that spells the method is counted -- the safe direction.  They see only the LITERAL `endswith(`: a space before
 466: #  the paren passes every one of them (hygienecensusholes e9).  `printf '%s\n'`, not `'%s'`: $(...) strips the list's last
 467: #  newline, and a line-reader then drops the last file -- measured by this round's own controls.
 468: _promptless=$(printf '%s\n' "$_ptylist" | python3 "$HERE/census_tokens.py" promptless "$HERE" | LC_ALL=C sort)
 469: _want=$(printf '%s' "$GRANDFATHERED_PROMPTLESS" | tr ' ' '
 470: ' | grep -c . )
 471: _got=$(printf '%s' "$_promptless" | grep -c . )
 472: 
 473: check "readiness: pty-probe denominator (enumerate FIRST)" "$_ptyn" "$EXPECT_PTY_PROBES"
 474: check "readiness: promptless probes, grandfathered set only" "$_got" "$_want"
 475: 
 476: #  Name the offender rather than only the count -- a bare number tells nobody
 477: #  WHICH file, and the whole point of the denominator is attribution.
 478: #  hygienecensus pass 2: AND GRADE THE NAMES, not only the count.  The count row alone read 42 == 42 after one
 479: #  grandfathered probe was swapped for a new promptless one (measured); the owner's ruling is "grandfather BY NAME",
 480: #  so the set is what is graded.  LC_ALL=C on both sides: comm needs one collation.
 481: _gfs=$(printf '%s' "$GRANDFATHERED_PROMPTLESS" | tr ' ' '\n' | grep . | LC_ALL=C sort)
 482: _new=$(LC_ALL=C comm -13 <(printf '%s\n' "$_gfs") <(printf '%s\n' "$_promptless" | grep .) 2>/dev/null | tr '\n' ' ' | sed 's/ $//')
 483: _gone=$(LC_ALL=C comm -23 <(printf '%s\n' "$_gfs") <(printf '%s\n' "$_promptless" | grep .) 2>/dev/null | tr '\n' ' ' | sed 's/ $//')
 484: check "readiness: promptless names == grandfathered names" "new=${_new:-none} gone=${_gone:-none}" "new=none gone=none"
 485: if [ -n "$_new" ]; then
 486:         echo "  NEW PROMPTLESS PTY PROBE(S) -- add prompt detection, or grandfather"
 487:         echo "  deliberately by editing GRANDFATHERED_PROMPTLESS above:"
 488:         printf '    %s
 489: ' $_new
 490: fi
 491: 
 492: #  THE POSITIVE HALF OF THE #392 CHECK, and it is the half that binds the offline
 493: #  truth table in gate_offline.sh to the code that actually ships.  Counting only
 494: #  the ABSENCE of the bad spelling is not enough: a site could be deleted, or
 495: #  reverted to something that is neither the old form nor the new one, and the two
 496: #  checks above would both stay green.  So count the three constructs the
 497: #  conversion introduced and require all fifteen of each.
 498: #
 499: #  Exact equality again, and for the same reason as EXPECT_BARE: a ">=" would let
 500: #  a site quietly disappear.  If a probe legitimately gains or loses a wait site,
 501: #  this number is edited deliberately, in the same commit, by someone who looked.
 502: #
 503: #  14 -> 15 on 2026-08-16: m8820x_sites_probe.py.  It shipped in 9494c6a with the
 504: #  anchored form and the echo guard but NOT the pinned call form -- so two of the
 505: #  three counts went to 15 and this gate went red, correctly, on a commit that had
 506: #  never been run past it.  The probe is converted rather than exempted, and the
 507: #  conversion was not free: reordering wait()'s parameters turned an existing
 508: #  positional wait(120) into mark=120, which is a real behaviour change and is
 509: #  fixed in the same commit.  That is the argument for the exact-equality pin.
 510: #  15 -> 16 on 2026-08-19: footbridge_sites_probe.py, the first reachability witness this tree
 511: #  has ever had for a footbridge machine.  MEASURED with this gate's own probe_code() census on
 512: #  a staged copy: conv_anchor, conv_echo and conv_mark all move 15 -> 16 while bare, unknown and
 513: #  whole_full do not move at all.  The probe ships WITH the pinned #392 call form rather than
 514: #  being converted afterwards -- which is the lesson of the m8820x entry above, where a probe
 515: #  landed in 9494c6a carrying two of the three constructs and turned this gate red on a commit
 516: #  that had never been run past it.
 517: #  16 -> 17 on 2026-08-19: luna_intmask_probe.py, #438's witness.  *** AND THIS ENTRY IS THE
 518: #  THIRD TIME THIS GATE HAS GONE RED FOR THE SAME REASON, ONE COMMIT AFTER THE SECOND. ***
 519: #  cf4b083 added the probe carrying all three pinned constructs and did NOT move this number.
 520: #  The commit ran gate 2 and the float selftest; GATE 6 NEVER FIRED, so the red was never seen
 521: #  and shipped latent -- it would have failed the Aug 22 weekly battery as a hygiene regression
 522: #  with a bookkeeping cause, which is the phantom-regression class this project already names.
 523: #  Found by the flagship seat recomputing this gate's own probe_code() census read-only.
 524: #
 525: #  The m8820x entry above records this shape, the footbridge entry directly above records it
 526: #  again, AND THE FOOTBRIDGE ENTRY WAS WRITTEN THE SAME DAY -- by the same operator, who was
 527: #  warned about the coupling for THAT probe, handled it correctly, and then repeated it for a
 528: #  different probe one commit later.  Reading a lesson is not the same as having it.
 529: #
 530: #  The structural fix, since three annotations have not been enough: a probe that ships in the
 531: #  same commit as its gate wiring cannot skip this pin, because the wiring forces a gate run.
 532: #  cf4b083 shipped the probe UNWIRED, so nothing made gate 6 fire.  See the luna88k block in
 533: #  gate_arm.sh, added with this bump.
 534: #  17 -> 18 on 2026-08-20: pit8253_latch_probe.py, #439's witness for pitlatch/pitclobber.
 535: #  MEASURED read-only with this gate's OWN probe_code()/py_code() definitions, run before and
 536: #  after staging the file: conv_anchor, conv_echo and conv_mark all move 17 -> 18 while bare,
 537: #  unknown and whole_full do not move at all (0 / 0 / 2 both times) -- the same signature the
 538: #  footbridge entry above records.  Bumped in the SAME edit that added the probe, which is the
 539: #  coupling the three entries above went red for.
 540: #  The probe ships UNWIRED.  Per the structural note directly above, that means nothing forces
 541: #  gate 6 to fire, and an unwired probe is exactly what shipped latent in cf4b083 -- so this pin
 542: #  is doing the whole job here and was verified by recomputation rather than by a gate run.
 543: #  Wiring it into gate_arm.sh alongside footbridge_sites_probe.py is the follow-up, and it wants
 544: #  an operator who can run that gate.
 545: #  18 -> 19 on 2026-08-20: sh4_bsc_width_probe.py, #441's detector for sh4bcr.  MEASURED the
 546: #  same way -- this gate's own probe_code()/py_code(), run before and after staging: all three of
 547: #  conv_anchor, conv_echo and conv_mark move 18 -> 19 while bare, unknown and whole_full do not
 548: #  move (0/0/2).  The same signature every entry above records.
 549: #
 550: #  IT DID NOT START THAT WAY, and the near-miss is the part worth keeping.  The probe first named
 551: #  its local `mark` rather than `_mark`, so the fresh-mark guard was PRESENT but invisible to
 552: #  conv_mark, which keys on `return wait(mark=_mark, ...)`.  Two counts went green and the third
 553: #  went red -- and the first draft of THIS comment argued that was correct and blamed the census,
 554: #  on the reasoning that renaming a local to satisfy a grep is the tail wagging the dog.  Wrong:
 555: #  `_mark` is the idiom in every other pty probe in the tree, so the new file was the outlier.
 556: #  Teaching the census a second spelling would have made it count substrings of the first.
 557: #  19 -> 20 on 2026-08-20: fbpending_drain_probe.py, #442's rung-3 reproduction.  All three
 558: #  counts move together after two corrections made in the same edit: the probe was renamed
 559: #  `mark` -> `_mark` (the sh4markspell shape, one round old), and conv_mark's pattern was
 560: #  extended to see the METHOD form, because this is the first class-based pty probe in the
 561: #  tree and its guard was invisible to a pattern written for closures.
 562: #  20 -> 21 on 2026-08-21: fbpending_witness.py, #442's rung-3 reproduction, COMMITTED as part
 563: #  of that round's pass 2.  It had lived in _scratchpad/, so a clone could not re-run the
 564: #  reproduction that licensed the fix -- the mrwstore2 shape, whose probe left no artefact at all.
 565: #  All three counts move together (21/21/21): it uses the closure form and the `_mark` spelling,
 566: #  so neither of the two corrections the previous entry needed applies here.
 567: #  21 -> 22 on 2026-08-21: sh4_pcic_probe.py, #443's detector.  All three counts move together
 568: #  (22/22/22) and bare/unknown/whole_full do not move (0/0/2) -- the signature every prior entry
 569: #  records.
 570: #
 571: #  *** THIS PIN WAS LATENTLY RED AT HEAD BETWEEN 3f9ff56 AND THIS COMMIT, AND THE CAUSE WAS A
 572: #  CONCURRENCY SLIP RATHER THAN AN OVERSIGHT ABOUT THE COUPLING. ***  A `git add -A regress/`
 573: #  during the #442 pass-2 commit swept in a probe a background agent was still creating, so the
 574: #  file landed WITHOUT its pin and WITHOUT this annotation.  That is the FOURTH recurrence of the
 575: #  coupling these comments already document (m8820x, footbridge, luna_intmask, sh4_bsc_width) --
 576: #  and the first caused by staging rather than by forgetting.
 577: #
 578: #  The structural note above says a probe shipping in the same commit as its gate wiring cannot
 579: #  skip the pin, because the wiring forces a gate run.  `git add -A` defeats that by adding files
 580: #  the author did not choose.  STAGE BY EXPLICIT PATH when a background agent may be writing into
 581: #  the same directory.
 582: #  22 -> 23 in the SAME commit: sh4pcic_witness.py, #443's rung-3 reproduction, committed
 583: #  alongside its detector.  It is a SEPARATE artefact from sh4_pcic_probe.py and both belong in
 584: #  the tree: the witness asserts the PRE-FIX symptom (so it correctly reads 15/30 now) and the
 585: #  detector asserts the FIXED behaviour.  Grading one by the other's clauses is a category error.
 586: #
 587: #  Committing it is the mrwstore2 lesson: that round's rung-3 probe left NO ARTEFACT, so its
 588: #  witness became a remembered grep and a later row had to re-cost the work.  #442's witness had
 589: #  the same problem and was committed one commit ago for the same reason.
 590: #  *** 23 -> 24 ON 2026-09-23, the same owner-ruled re-derivation.  luna_absentcpu_probe.py (committed
 591: #  79cd12e, 2026-09-06) is anchored and echo-guarded, so those two censuses counted it (24) -- but its
 592: #  send() spelled the fresh mark `mark=mark`, which the fresh-mark census does not read, so that one said
 593: #  23 and the three #392 counts DISAGREED at HEAD.  The probe's rename to `_mark` (same change) makes the
 594: #  three agree; the deriver refuses to write while they disagree, which is why the rename precedes the pin. ***
 595: EXPECT_CONVERTED=24
 596: #  One helper for all three, so they agree on WHAT they look at.  The first draft
 597: #  used py_code() for the anchored count and a raw grep for the other two, which
 598: #  meant a comment mentioning the echo guard would have inflated one count and not
 599: #  the others -- a spurious mismatch with no defect behind it.  A seat caught it
 600: #  while it was still latent.
 601: #  Same scope as py_code above -- the two must agree, and a past defect came from
 602: #  their populations diverging.
 603: probe_code() { find "$HERE" -name '*.py' -type f ! -path "$HERE/artifacts/*" \
 604:                     ! -name 'readiness_predicate_test.py' -print0 2>/dev/null | \
 605:                xargs -0 grep -h "$1" 2>/dev/null | grep -v '^[[:space:]]*#'; }
 606: conv_anchor=$(probe_code 'resp.rstrip().endswith("GXemul>")' | grep -c .)
 607: conv_echo=$(probe_code 'echo is not None and echo not in resp' | grep -c .)
 608: #  ANCHOR THROUGH THE ECHO ARGUMENT.  The first version stopped at
 609: #  'return wait(mark=_mark' -- a PREFIX -- so deleting ", echo=s if s else None"
 610: #  from all fourteen sites left this count at 14 and every other counter
 611: #  unchanged. A pass-2 seat MEASURED that mutant passing gate_hygiene,
 612: #  gate_offline, gate_mips_rounding and gate_sh_rounding simultaneously: a
 613: #  shipped fix with no detector, which is this project's worst vacuity class.
 614: #  The comma is what makes the assertion an assertion.
 615: #  ANCHOR THROUGH THE WHOLE EXPRESSION, not just as far as "echo=". A pass-2b
 616: #  seat pointed out that the previous form still admitted
 617: #      return wait(mark=_mark, echo=None)
 618: #  which passes the argument and disables the guard in ONE WORD. This is the
 619: #  THIRD tightening of this same count -- first it stopped at "_mark", then at
 620: #  "echo=", now at the full conditional. Each time the surviving prefix was a
 621: #  real substring of both the fixed and the broken code, which is exactly the
 622: #  property a detector must not have.
 623: #  *** EXTENDED 2026-08-20 FOR THE METHOD FORM, and narrowly on purpose given the history
 624: #  in the paragraph above. ***  Every probe until now wrote `send()` as a closure over a local
 625: #  `buf`, so the guard read `return wait(mark=_mark, ...)`.  fbpending_drain_probe.py is the
 626: #  first CLASS-BASED probe in the tree: its guard is `return self.wait(mark=_mark, ..., 
 627: #  timeout=timeout)`, which is not a spelling variation but a different construct, and the
 628: #  pattern could not see it AT ALL -- the guard was present, correct, and uncounted.
 629: #
 630: #  That is a wider blind spot than the `mark`/`_mark` one filed as sh4markspell: a whole probe
 631: #  STYLE was invisible.  The two additions here are `\(self\.\)\?` and an optional trailing
 632: #  argument.  Both are anchored -- `mark=_mark` and the full `echo=` conditional still have to
 633: #  be present and in order -- so neither re-opens the hole the three earlier tightenings closed:
 634: #  a site that passes the argument, or drops the conditional, still fails to match.
 635: conv_mark=$(probe_code 'return \(self\.\)\?wait(mark=_mark, echo=[a-z][a-z]* if [a-z][a-z]* else None[,)]' | grep -c .)
 636: 
 637: #  THE HOLE A PASS-2 SEAT FOUND, and it is the sharpest finding of the review:
 638: #  the checks above catch a REVERT but not an ADDITION of the OTHER broken form.
 639: #  A brand-new site spelled
 640: #        if buf.rstrip().endswith("GXemul>"):
 641: #  passes everything -- bare is still 0, the spelling is RECOGNISED so `unknown`
 642: #  stays 0, and it is not the anchored form so conv_anchor stays 14.  Yet #392
 643: #  MEASURED that exact configuration (full prompt, whole buffer) failing just as
 644: #  completely as the bare one: arm B scored 0/80, byte-identical to arm A.
 645: #
 646: #  So count it explicitly.  The two that exist are arm_flags_probe.py:144 and
 647: #  :645 -- known, filed, and deliberately out of #392's scope.  Pinning the count
 648: #  at 2 turns that scope decision into a FAIL-CLOSED ALLOWLIST: those two are
 649: #  tolerated, a third is not, and when they are finally converted this number
 650: #  goes to 0 in the same commit.
 651: #  *** 2 -> 3 ON 2026-09-23 BY OWNER RULING, AGAINST THIS BLOCK'S OWN DEFAULT -- recorded as an EXCEPTION,
 652: #  not a precedent.  The third site is arm_bdt_probe.py:76 (committed 4dfc716, #524, 2026-09-12): its
 653: #  `self.buf.rstrip().endswith("GXemul>")' raised the count to 3 in a commit that did not move this pin,
 654: #  so this row was red at HEAD.  The block below says a new hit is never answered by raising the pin; the
 655: #  owner chose to allowlist it BY NAME rather than convert it inside the census round.  Converting its
 656: #  readiness to #392's anchored form is FILED (`armbdtreadiness`).  A count alone would let a site MOVE
 657: #  between files unseen, so a second row pins each FILE WITH ITS OWN COUNT (WHOLE_FULL_FILES, `name:count`,
 658: #  sorted under LC_ALL=C).  Pass 2 of this round MEASURED two holes in the first form of these rows (a set of
 659: #  names let a site move between the two files; the pattern matched only the double-quoted spelling), and the
 660: #  F2 pass two more in the repair (the rows counted LINES, so two sites on one line read as one, and a site kept
 661: #  as a trailing comment after its conversion still counted).  Both rows now come from census_tokens.py: code
 662: #  tokens only, either quote, any spacing, every OCCURRENCE.  Other spellings of the same test -- another
 663: #  receiver name, `strip()`, a slice compare -- are the filed `hygienecensusholes`, not closed here. ***
 664: WHOLE_FULL_FILES="arm_bdt_probe.py:1 arm_flags_probe.py:2"
 665: EXPECT_WHOLE_FULL=3
 666: #  *** ROW 3: SCOPED TO FILES THAT CAN DRIVE A REAL GUEST, rather than exempting a
 667: #  filename.  Adjudicated 2026-08-29; the name-exclusion form was WITHDRAWN. ***
 668: #
 669: #  The allowlist below is FAIL-CLOSED over a pattern #392 MEASURED at 0/80, so the
 670: #  disposition for a new hit is never to raise the pin.  The two that appeared are
 671: #  `selftest_inactivity.py:87` -- a deliberately preserved negative control whose own
 672: #  docstring says DO NOT FIX IT -- and `:102`, #467's correct shape.  NEITHER DRIVES A
 673: #  GUEST: both read a Stub that pops a python list, so #392's failure mode (a real
 674: #  guest's own output racing the predicate) cannot arise.  That file is also LIVE
 675: #  APPARATUS -- gate_offline.sh runs it every battery -- so it must not be edited.
 676: #
 677: #  A filename exemption was the wrong shape and this file says so at :92-95 and
 678: #  :347-348: an exemption must travel WITH a content pin on the excluded file.  Scoping
 679: #  the POPULATION needs neither an exemption nor a companion pin, and it subsumes the
 680: #  readiness_predicate_test.py case automatically.
 681: #
 682: #  THE POPULATION TERM IS FOUR ALTERNATIVES -- pty.fork, forkpty, pty.spawn, importing drive_guest (it said
 683: #  "TWO" until 2026-09-23) -- and the drive_guest one is not hypothetical:
 684: #  `selftest_absorb.py`, `selftest_budget.py` and `selftest_logdir.py` import
 685: #  drive_guest and contain NO pty.fork literal -- measured -- so a pty.fork-only term
 686: #  would let a guest-driving file out of the census.  None holds a whole-full site
 687: #  today; the term is widened so that it cannot.
 688: #
 689: #  *** THE CLAIM IS SCOPED, NOT GENERAL, AND TWO SEATS SPLIT ON IT. ***  codex named
 690: #  three files that drive the emulator with NEITHER spelling -- hpcabort_witness.py,
 691: #  hpcmips_ctor_probe.py and sgi_eaddr_probe.py, through subprocess.run or GDB --args.
 692: #  So this is NOT a general guest-driving census and must not be read as one.  grok
 693: #  argued against widening to cover them and that is the disposition taken: they
 694: #  cannot carry the #392 failure, which is a PTY READINESS RACE, and all three hold
 695: #  zero `endswith(` in any case (measured).  Widening to processes that cannot exhibit
 696: #  the defect would make this pin answer a question it is not asking.
 697: #
 698: #  DISCLOSED RESIDUALS, all real, none live today: `from pty import fork` with a bare
 699: #  fork(); `import pty as p`; a statement-local import; `import regress.drive_guest`.  Censused at 0
 700: #  apiece.  (`os.forkpty` was listed here too until 2026-09-23 -- the pattern MATCHES it.)  THE
 701: #  PRESENT VALUE (3, pinned above) IS CORRECT; THAT DOES NOT ESTABLISH POPULATION COMPLETENESS.
 702: #
 703: #  `-print0 | xargs -0` matches both neighbours: a bare xargs finds ZERO files when a
 704: #  path contains a space (measured).  No space in $HERE today; the neighbours' own
 705: #  comment says the three must agree, so it agrees.
 706: #
 707: #  NO FILENAME EXEMPTION HERE.  readiness_predicate_test.py matches neither alternative
 708: #  and holds zero whole-full sites, so scoping subsumes it -- an earlier draft carried
 709: #  the exemption anyway, inert and un-companioned by a content pin, which is the shape
 710: #  :92-95 condemns.  opus5 found it dead and it is gone.
 711: pty_driving() { find "$HERE" -name '*.py' -type f ! -path "$HERE/artifacts/*" \
 712:                      -print0 2>/dev/null \
 713:                 | xargs -0 grep -lE 'pty\.fork|forkpty|pty\.spawn|^[[:space:]]*(import|from) drive_guest' 2>/dev/null; }
 714: pty_code() { pty_driving | tr '\n' '\0' \
 715:              | xargs -0 grep -h "$1" 2>/dev/null | grep -v '^[[:space:]]*#'; }
 716: #  hygienecensus 2026-09-23: the allowlist BY NAME, WITH EACH FILE'S OWN COUNT -- `path:count` for every file
 717: #  holding a CODE-token whole-buffer site (census_tokens.py; per occurrence), over the same pty-driving
 718: #  population, paths relative to regress/, sorted under LC_ALL=C and space-joined.  The count is their sum.
 719: whole_files=$(pty_driving | sed "s|^$HERE/||" | python3 "$HERE/census_tokens.py" whole "$HERE" | LC_ALL=C sort | tr '\n' ' ' | sed 's/ $//')
 720: whole_full=$(printf '%s\n' $whole_files | awk -F: 'NF > 1 {s += $NF} END {print s + 0}')
 721: check "readiness: whole-buffer full-prompt sites (allowlist)" "$whole_full" "$EXPECT_WHOLE_FULL"
 722: check "readiness: whole-buffer sites, per allowlisted file" "$whole_files" "$WHOLE_FULL_FILES"
 723: check "readiness: anchored full-prompt sites (#392)" "$conv_anchor" "$EXPECT_CONVERTED"
 724: check "readiness: echo guard present (#392)"         "$conv_echo"   "$EXPECT_CONVERTED"
 725: check "readiness: send takes a fresh mark (#392)"    "$conv_mark"   "$EXPECT_CONVERTED"
 726: 
 727: #  PIN THE EXEMPTED FILE'S OWN CONTENTS.  readiness_predicate_test.py is excluded
 728: #  from the census above because it deliberately contains the broken spellings;
 729: #  that exemption is only safe while the file still HOLDS them.  If its two
 730: #  negative arms were deleted the offline truth table would go green by testing
 731: #  nothing, and the exclusion above would hide it.  So the exemption and this
 732: #  assertion travel together -- an allowlist entry that checks what it exempts.
 733: #  -o again, not -c: OCCURRENCES, not lines.  Same reason as above, and the
 734: #  expected numbers are asymmetric for a real reason -- 2 bad arms, but 3 good
 735: #  occurrences, because besides the two `full-*` arms the test also prints the
 736: #  LEFTOVER demonstration line, which re-matches the prompt on purpose to show
 737: #  that rstrip() erases the trailing space.  The first draft of this check
 738: #  expected 2 and failed; the file was right and the expectation was wrong.
 739: #  CODE ONLY here too.  The first version grepped the file RAW, so a seat
 740: #  gutted both negative arms, dropped the two spellings into a single COMMENT
 741: #  line, and the pin still read 2 and 3. The filter that this file already
 742: #  applies sixty lines above was missing from the one check whose whole job is
 743: #  to keep an exemption honest.
 744: rpt="$HERE/readiness_predicate_test.py"
 745: rpt_code() { [ -f "$rpt" ] && grep -v '^[[:space:]]*#' "$rpt"; }
 746: check "readiness: truth table keeps its 2 bad arms" \
 747:       "$( [ -f "$rpt" ] && rpt_code | grep -o 'endswith(">")' | wc -l || echo missing)" "2"
 748: #  FIVE, not three: the two original full-* arms, the two late-prompt arms that
 749: #  give the ECHO conjunct its behavioural coverage, and the leftover demo.
 750: check "readiness: truth table keeps its good arms + leftover demo" \
 751:       "$( [ -f "$rpt" ] && rpt_code | grep -o 'endswith("GXemul>")' | wc -l || echo missing)" "5"
 752: 
 753: #  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 754: #  A CLASS RATCHET, not a bug fix.  Task from the R6 pass-2 kimi seat, whose
 755: #  answer had gone unread for a day: "sweep for other divisions in
 756: #  debug()/fatal() argument position with guest-writable operands -- ns16550 is
 757: #  unlikely to be the only instance of that shape."
 758: #
 759: #  The shape is worth a ratchet because C evaluates an argument whether or not
 760: #  the callee prints it, so a division there runs at EVERY verbosity.  A grep
 761: #  cannot find it: the operator has to be in argument position with literals and
 762: #  comments already stripped, and the call may span lines.  diag_div_sweep.py
 763: #  balances the parens and does that; it also splits constant divisors (which
 764: #  cannot be zero, so are not the shape) from drivable ones.
 765: #
 766: #  MEASURED 2026-08-16: 24 sites, 22 constant, 2 non-constant -- so kimi's
 767: #  hypothesis is REFUTED, and the ratchet is what keeps the refutation true.
 768: #    * dev_ns16550.c, anchor `115200 / d->divisor` -- the live one, filed `ns16550`.
 769: #      The other 115200 in that file is `115200 / 9600`, a CONSTANT divisor and not
 770: #      the shape, so anchor on the `d->divisor` form rather than on the number.
 771: #    * diskimage.c, anchor `(int) (bytes_left % ct->sector_size)` -- NOT drivable:
 772: #      every unsupported TRACK mode reaches a `goto fail` immediately following the
 773: #      diagnostic anchored at `unsupported TRACK mode`, and `nr_of_pt ++` runs only
 774: #      after that, so a counted track always carries 2048/2336/2352.  The plain
 775: #      division -- anchor `nr_sectors = bytes_left / ct->sector_size` -- would fault
 776: #      first regardless.
 777: #
 778: #  *** THESE WERE LINE NUMBERS UNTIL 2026-08-30 AND THREE OF THE FIVE HAD DRIFTED. ***
 779: #  `dev_ns16550.c:79` had become a comment line (the division is at :181);
 780: #  `diskimage.c:543` was wrong by 440 lines (:983); and `:819` named the LAST ARGUMENT
 781: #  LINE of the diagnostic rather than the `goto fail` below it -- the
 782: #  read-the-line-before-citing-it trap, inside a citation whose only job is to be
 783: #  re-checked.  `:823` and `:974` were still correct, so this is drift and not a bad
 784: #  transcription.  Every anchor above was verified to match EXACTLY ONCE when written;
 785: #  the bare form `bytes_left % ct->sector_size` matches TWICE and is deliberately not
 786: #  used.  An anchor that stops matching is LOUD; a drifted number is silent.
 787: #  A THIRD site means someone added a new instance of the class: read it before
 788: #  raising this number.
 789: DIVSWEEP=$HERE/diag_div_sweep.py
 790: if [ -f "$DIVSWEEP" ] && command -v python3 > /dev/null 2>&1; then
 791:     dsout=$(python3 "$DIVSWEEP" 2>/dev/null)
 792:     check "class ratchet: drivable divisions in diagnostic argument position" \
 793:           "$(printf '%s\n' "$dsout" | grep -oE 'DIAG_DIV_NONCONST=[0-9]+' | cut -d= -f2)" "2"
 794:     #  The denominator too: if the sweep silently stopped finding ANYTHING the
 795:     #  count above would still read 2 only by luck, and a zero total is the
 796:     #  classic vacuous green.
 797:     #  *** 24 -> 21 ON 2026-08-29, AND IT IS A CONSOLIDATION, NOT THREE REMOVALS. ***
 798:     #  NOT ONE INSTANCE OF THE CLASS LEFT THE CODE.  `7c9dd9e` (#462) merged FOUR
 799:     #  one-division fatal() calls -- grep anchor `dma_reg[4 / sizeof(uint32_t)]` --
 800:     #  into ONE fatal() carrying the SAME FOUR DIVISIONS as its arguments.
 801:     #  DIAG_DIV_TOTAL counts CALLS carrying at least one division, so merging four
 802:     #  into one moves the total while the division census is unchanged.  A round
 803:     #  acting on the first reading would have hunted three removals that never were.
 804:     #
 805:     #  Attribution settled BY MEASUREMENT, because two seats disagreed (#490 vs #462):
 806:     #      git show 7c9dd9e --format= -- <file> | grep -cE '^[+-].*dma_reg'  ->  9
 807:     #      (the BARE `| grep -c dma_reg` form yields 11 -- it counts context
 808:     #      lines too.  9 is the CHANGED-LINES count and is the right figure;
 809:     #      the command printed here was the wrong one.  Caught by fable5.)
 810:     #      git show fb0cc78 --format= -- <file> | grep -cE '^[+-].*dma_reg'  ->  0
 811:     #
 812:     #  *** CITED BY COMMIT AND ANCHOR, NOT BY LINE NUMBER, AND DELIBERATELY. ***  This
 813:     #  sweep computes its printed line numbers from STRIPPED-text offsets indexed
 814:     #  against the RAW file, so they run systematically small -- it reports the gdrom
 815:     #  sites 400+ lines early, landing inside a comment block.  Its COUNTS do not
 816:     #  depend on that and are sound.  Filed as `divsweeplines`; the remedy (a
 817:     #  length-preserving strip) is measured to move the citations to their true
 818:     #  call-start lines with the counts unchanged.  Do not paste its line numbers.
 819:     #
 820:     #  THE DOWN DIRECTION NEEDED ITS OWN INSTRUCTION AND NOW HAS ONE.  The rule above
 821:     #  ("A THIRD site means someone added a new instance") is written for GROWTH.  A
 822:     #  DECREASE is ambiguous: it can mean the code improved, or that the sweep quietly
 823:     #  stopped finding things -- the vacuous-green shape this denominator exists to
 824:     #  catch.  THE DISCRIMINATOR IS THE NUMERATOR: if DIAG_DIV_NONCONST still reads 2
 825:     #  and names the same two sites, the sweep works and a lower total is real.  If
 826:     #  the numerator moves too, suspect the TOOL before the code.  Then diff the site
 827:     #  list against the last green tree AT FILE GRANULARITY, the lines being wrong.
 828:     #
 829:     #  FILED, NOT TAKEN HERE: a seat argued this should become a FLOOR, since an
 830:     #  equality breaks on every legitimate de-fatalisation and de-fatalisation is this
 831:     #  fork's whole queue.  The argument is good.  Converting it changes what the
 832:     #  check PROVES, so it is not folded into a re-pin.
 833:     check "class ratchet: the sweep still sees the whole call set" \
 834:           "$(printf '%s\n' "$dsout" | grep -oE 'DIAG_DIV_TOTAL=[0-9]+' | cut -d= -f2)" "21"
 835: else
 836:     note "diag_div_sweep.py or python3 missing -- class ratchet NOT run"
 837: fi
 838: 
 839: PLOG=$LOGDIR/pmax.ptylog
 840: ALOG=$LOGDIR/arc.ptylog
 841: [ -f "$PLOG" ] && [ -f "$ALOG" ] || \
 842:     gate_skip "no logs from this run -- gate 4 must run first (it publishes them here)"
 843: 
 844: # ---- positive control --------------------------------------------------
 845: # Without this the gate cannot tell "nothing went wrong" from "nothing happened".
 846: # The floor is MEASURED, not guessed. A healthy run produces about 4,600 bytes on pmax
 847: # (a plain serial console) and about 1,195,000 on arc (a VGA text console that repaints
 848: # differentially, so it re-emits the screen constantly). An earlier draft used a single
 849: # 20,000-byte floor for both and failed pmax on a perfectly good boot -- a false red, which
 850: # is how gates get switched off. 2,000 is comfortably below the smaller rig and still far
 851: # above the "empty or truncated file" case this control exists to catch.
 852: for pair in "pmax:$PLOG" "arc:$ALOG"; do
 853:     lab=${pair%%:*}; f=${pair##*:}
 854:     check_min "$lab: log is substantial"   "$(wc -c < "$f")" 2000
 855: done
 856: # pmax sets the 8th bit on every console character; arc repaints differentially. Strip the
 857: # high bit for pmax and accept either the raw arc log or gate 4's screen rebuild.
 858: check "pmax: log proves a real boot (uid=0)" \
 859:       "$(tr '\200-\377' '\000-\177' < "$PLOG" | grep -qa 'uid=0(root)' && echo yes || echo no)" "yes"
 860: check "arc: log proves a real boot (uid=0)" \
 861:       "$( { grep -qa 'uid=0(root)' "$ALOG" || grep -qa 'uid=0(root)' "$LOGDIR/arc.screen" 2>/dev/null; } && echo yes || echo no)" "yes"
 862: echo
 863: 
 864: # ---- markers that must be ZERO -----------------------------------------
 865: # Every pattern below was verified to exist as an emittable string in src/. Three did NOT
 866: # survive that check and were removed rather than left in place looking like coverage:
 867: #   'FIFO underrun'        -- 0 sites. The real string is 'FIFO overrun' (4 sites).
 868: #   'INSTR: unimplemented' -- 0 sites. The token 'INSTR:' is in no format string at all;
 869: #                             the real dyntrans marker is 'could not find physical'.
 870: #   '{ asc: data in'       -- exists, but is NOT distress. See the bounded list below.
 871: PATTERNS='panic
 872: FATAL
 873: zero-length
 874: FIFO overrun
 875: short DATA_IN DMA
 876: short DATA_OUT DMA
 877: unimplemented format
 878: could not find physical'
 879: 
 880: printf "  %-28s %8s %8s\n" "marker" "pmax" "arc"
 881: while IFS= read -r pat; do
 882:     [ -n "$pat" ] || continue
 883:     p=$(count "$PLOG" "$pat")
 884:     a=$(count "$ALOG" "$pat")
 885:     printf "  %-28s %8s %8s\n" "$pat" "$p" "$a"
 886:     check "pmax clean: $pat" "$p" "0"
 887:     check "arc  clean: $pat" "$a" "0"
 888: done <<EOF
 889: $PATTERNS
 890: EOF
 891: echo
 892: 
 893: # ---- the OTHER rigs' logs ----------------------------------------------
 894: # This gate used to read only the two MIPS pty logs, so a panic in the m88k or SuperH
 895: # guest left it green while the README claimed "any boot log". Gate 5's logs are scanned
 896: # here too when they exist -- they are the newer, less-exercised rigs, which is exactly
 897: # where a distress marker is most likely to be informative.
 898: for rig in luna88k landisk; do
 899:     rlog=$LOGDIR/drive_$rig.log
 900:     [ -f "$rlog" ] || { note "$rig: no log from this run (gate 5 did not run it)"; continue; }
 901:     while IFS= read -r pat; do
 902:         [ -n "$pat" ] || continue
 903:         c=$(count "$rlog" "$pat")
 904:         [ "$c" != 0 ] && printf "  %-20s %-28s %s\n" "$rig" "$pat" "$c"
 905:         check "$rig clean: $pat" "$c" "0"
 906:     done <<EOF
 907: $PATTERNS
 908: EOF
 909: done
 910: echo
 911: 
 912: # ---- markers with a KNOWN NON-ZERO baseline ----------------------------
 913: # '{ asc: data in, lenIn=.. lenIn2=.. }' (dev_asc.c:417) fires whenever a SCSI target has
 914: # fewer bytes ready than the guest asked for. That is an ordinary occurrence -- measured
 915: # at 1 on pmax and 5 on arc across a healthy OpenBSD 2.2 boot -- so demanding zero was
 916: # simply wrong, and this gate failed on it the first time it ran.
 917: #
 918: # It is still worth watching, because the failure mode that matters is a FLOOD: round 40
 919: # found #265's own diagnostic firing on every healthy boot, and that showed up only as a
 920: # marker count. So the assertion is a CEILING rather than an equality -- generous enough
 921: # that ordinary boot-to-boot variation cannot trip it, tight enough that a per-transfer
 922: # flood will.
 923: printf "  %-28s %8s %8s   %s\n" "bounded marker" "pmax" "arc" "ceiling"
 924: BOUNDED_CEILING=50
 925: for pat in '{ asc: data in'; do
 926:     p=$(count "$PLOG" "$pat")
 927:     a=$(count "$ALOG" "$pat")
 928:     printf "  %-28s %8s %8s   %s\n" "$pat" "$p" "$a" "$BOUNDED_CEILING"
 929:     check "pmax not flooding: $pat" "$([ "$p" -le "$BOUNDED_CEILING" ] && echo ok || echo FLOOD)" "ok"
 930:     check "arc  not flooding: $pat" "$([ "$a" -le "$BOUNDED_CEILING" ] && echo ok || echo FLOOD)" "ok"
 931: done
 932: 
 933: gate_end
 934: exit $?

   1: #!/bin/bash
   2: # GATE 6 -- no distress markers in the boot logs.
   3: #
   4: # Cheap, and it has caught real regressions: round 40 found that #265's own diagnostic
   5: # flooded the log on a healthy boot, and that #261 was listing a subsystem it had not
   6: # actually armed. Neither changed a pass/fail count -- both showed up only as marker counts.
   7: #
   8: # READS THE RAW PTY LOGS ON PURPOSE. fatal() and debugmsg() output is not guest console
   9: # output; it goes straight to stdout and interleaves into the pty stream, and the arc
  10: # screen reconstruction that gate 4 needs for GUEST tokens actively garbles it. Measured:
  11: # '{ asc: data in' reads 0 from the arc screen rebuild and 5 from the raw log.
  12: #
  13: # TWO WAYS THIS GATE USED TO PASS WITHOUT PROVING ANYTHING, both now closed:
  14: #
  15: #  * EMPTY LOGS. It checked only that the files existed. `: > /tmp/pD.log` made 20 checks
  16: #    go green -- two empty files certifying a clean boot. There is now a POSITIVE CONTROL:
  17: #    each log must be substantial AND contain the guest's own uid=0(root), so absence of
  18: #    distress is only counted when presence of a real boot is proved.
  19: #  * STALE LOGS. Everything lives in /tmp and survives until reboot, so running this gate
  20: #    alone -- or after gate 4 skipped -- graded yesterday's logs, produced by a binary that
  21: #    no longer exists. Gate 4 now deletes them first and copies them into $LOGDIR when it
  22: #    finishes; this gate reads THOSE copies, which only exist if gate 4 actually ran.
  23: set -u
  24: HERE=$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)
  25: . "$HERE/lib.sh"
  26: 
  27: gate_begin "log-hygiene"
  28: 
  29: # ---- static wiring: the readiness-predicate ratchet ---------------------
  30: #
  31: #  DELIBERATELY BEFORE the log check below, so a missing log can never SKIP
  32: #  it.  This gate had no source assertions at all until now -- it graded logs
  33: #  and nothing else -- which is why the defect it guards could spread unseen.
  34: #
  35: #  THE DEFECT IT FREEZES: a readiness predicate matching a BARE ">" is
  36: #  satisfied by the guest's own register dump, whose FIRST line is the
  37: #  symbol line "  <...>" -- and that line ends in ">".  Line-buffered on a
  38: #  pty, each line is its own flush, so a reader waking between line 1 and
  39: #  line 2 returns SATISFIED WITH THE REGISTERS UNREAD.  Healthy host,
  40: #  microseconds wide, and armed on every CPU family whose dump starts that
  41: #  way (MIPS, PPC, SH, m88k all do).
  42: #
  43: #  THE CONVERSION HAS LANDED (#392), so EXPECT_BARE is now 0 and this is a
  44: #  RATCHET AGAINST REGRESSION rather than against spread.  All 15 sites now take
  45: #  a fresh mark before the write, require the FULL prompt in that slice, and
  46: #  require the command's own echo first.
  47: #
  48: #  The converted predicate deliberately keeps the LITERAL endswith("GXemul>")
  49: #  spelling, which is the idiom the ARM probes already use (arm_idle_probe.py:194).
  50: #  A reviewer proposed a shared helper holding the prompt in a constant or a
  51: #  regex; either would have broken this gate, though -- as a pass-2 seat pointed
  52: #  out -- NOT by the same route, and the first draft of this comment conflated
  53: #  them:
  54: #      endswith(PROMPT)  -> counted, unrecognised -> fails the `unknown` check;
  55: #      re.search(...)    -> not an endswith at all, so `unknown` never moves --
  56: #                           it fails the anchored-site COUNT below instead.
  57: #  Matching the house idiom kept the ratchet working with no redesign, which is
  58: #  worth remembering the next time a "cleaner" abstraction is proposed for
  59: #  something a static check is watching.
  60: #
  61: #  Exact equality, not a ceiling, and on purpose: a "<=" check would let the
  62: #  number fall silently, and a silent fall is how you lose track of whether
  63: #  the conversion actually happened.  Any movement in either direction must
  64: #  be a deliberate edit here.
  65: #  COUNTING IT PROPERLY, after a review pass named three ways to walk around a
  66: #  naive grep: the single-quoted spelling, a variable or helper holding the
  67: #  prompt, and probes living in a subdirectory the flat glob never visits.
  68: #  A ratchet you can step around is worse than no ratchet, because it reads as
  69: #  protection.  So:
  70: #    - find RECURSIVELY, not "$HERE"/*.py;
  71: #    - accept either quote style;
  72: #    - and make the check CLOSED-WORLD: every .endswith() in the tree must use
  73: #      one of the two KNOWN literals.  A third spelling -- endswith(PROMPT),
  74: #      say -- is unknown, and unknown fails.  A helper module under regress/
  75: #      is scanned like a probe: an endswith( line there with a known literal
  76: #      is not "unknown" (the bare one still fails the bare row), and one with
  77: #      an unrecognised argument fails "unknown".  What no count here
  78: #      sees is WHICH probe a helper's test serves: a probe whose ONLY test
  79: #      moved into a helper is listed promptless and the names row reds
  80: #      (fail-closed), while a probe that keeps one call of its own can move
  81: #      the rest into a helper with every row green (hygienecensusholes n3k,
  82: #      n3k2; this said a helper's line was unknown until the census F3 pass,
  83: #      codex and grok).  That converts "I grepped for the bad thing" into "I
  84: #      enumerated everything and recognised all of it" -- for the spellings
  85: #      these counts can SEE.
  86: #      (*** 2026-09-23: this said it was "the only form that cannot be evaded
  87: #      by inventing a new way to write it".  FALSE: every count here is LINE-
  88: #      based and keys on the literal `endswith(`, so a space before the paren
  89: #      (`endswith (">")`) or a backslash continuation between the name and
  90: #      the paren removes a call from all of them (both measured on copies,
  91: #      controls_f2 e_e9 and controls_c5 t_bsl).  Filed as hygienecensusholes
  92: #      e9, ranked first; the census F3 pass widened it from one spelling to
  93: #      this LINE-based class (glm, agy, the kimi substitute).  And the
  94: #      comment filter drops every PHYSICAL line that begins with `#`: code on
  95: #      such a line (after a closing triple quote, or in an f-string field) and
  96: #      a whole file saved with CR-only line endings (grep then sees one line,
  97: #      beginning `#!`) escape too -- measured, the census F4 pass (the Opus
  98: #      seat: hash_line, cr_only), filed with e10.  The census is closed-world
  99: #      over the literal spellings on the physical lines it keeps, not over
 100: #      the method. ***)
 101: EXPECT_BARE=0
 102: EXPECT_UNKNOWN=0
 103: #  CODE ONLY.  The first version of this check counted a line of PROSE -- a
 104: #  comment in arm_flags_probe.py that discusses endswith() -- and reported an
 105: #  unrecognised spelling that does not exist.  A check that fires on its own
 106: #  documentation trains the reader to ignore it, so full-line comments are
 107: #  stripped before anything is counted.  (Known limit, stated rather than
 108: #  hidden: an endswith() sitting after code on the same line as a trailing
 109: #  comment is still counted as code, which is the safe direction.)
 110: #  readiness_predicate_test.py is EXCLUDED here and pinned separately below.
 111: #  It is the offline truth table for #392, so it necessarily CONTAINS the two
 112: #  broken spellings -- demonstrating them is its whole job. Counting them with

 114: #
 115: #  This is a FAIL-CLOSED exception, not a hole: the file is named exactly, and
 116: #  its own contents are asserted below, so it cannot quietly stop testing what it
 117: #  claims to test. A loose `grep -v test` would have been the wrong shape -- it
 118: #  would silently exempt any future file with "test" in its name.
 119: #  *** SCOPE, MEASURED 2026-08-29: `regress/artifacts/` IS PRESERVED EVIDENCE, NOT LIVE
 120: #  HARNESS CODE, and leaving it in scope was a defect in BOTH directions. ***
 121: #

 456: #  hygienecensus (2026-09-23): THE CLASSIFIER READS PYTHON TOKENS -- regress/census_tokens.py, where a COMMENT, a
 457: #  STRING or a DOCSTRING is not code.  It was a raw `grep -L "endswith("`, so text naming the method hid a
 458: #  promptless probe: crime_width_probe.py's only such text was a comment this very round added (pass 2).  The
 459: #  first repair stripped only FULL-LINE comments and said it "reads code" -- the F2 pass measured seven edits, on
 460: #  five probe files, where a TRAILING comment, a string or a docstring changed the answer (the Opus seat's n1a-n1d,
 461: #  n2a, n2b, n9).  A comment, a string or a docstring is never a "." NAME "(" sequence of tokens, so none of
 462: #  those can hide a probe now; and the classifier keeps STATEMENT boundaries, so a method reference on one line
 463: #  and a parenthesised string on the next is not a call (the closing pass, codex -- controls_c2/t_nljoin).  A file that cannot be read or tokenized is listed promptless (it cannot
 464: #  be read, so it is not trusted).  The spelling counts above deliberately stay textual, so a comment or a string
 465: #  that spells the method is counted -- the safe direction.  They see only the LITERAL `endswith(`: a space before
 466: #  the paren passes every one of them (hygienecensusholes e9).  `printf '%s\n'`, not `'%s'`: $(...) strips the list's last
 467: #  newline, and a line-reader then drops the last file -- measured by this round's own controls.

def without_doc(s):
    tree=ast.parse(s)
    if isinstance(tree.body[0],ast.Expr) and isinstance(tree.body[0].value,ast.Constant):
        tree.body=tree.body[1:]
    return ast.dump(tree,include_attributes=False)
print('classifier executable AST F3 equals F5:',without_doc(a)==without_doc(b))
